However, I haven't had any trouble sleeping, major concentration issues other than just feeling mentally tired, or any thoughts of hurting myself.\n\nDoctor: How has your self-esteem been during this time, and do you ever feel a sense of hopelessness about the future?\n\nPatient: I haven't really thought much about my self-esteem specifically. Mostly, I just feel completely empty and sad all the time, and I have absolutely no motivation to do anything.\n\nDoctor: Did this low mood start after a specific stressful event, loss, or major life change about a year and a half ago?\n\nPatient: Yes, it definitely started about a year and a half ago. However, I can't recall any specific stressful event, loss, or major life change that triggered it. It just seemed to come on gradually without any obvious reason.\n\nDoctor: How have these feelings and the lack of energy affected your daily life, such as your work, hobbies, or relationships with family and friends?\n\nPatient: I've completely withdrawn from my family and friends because even basic social interactions feel too exhausting to handle. I've also lost all interest in my hobbies, and I struggle to find any motivation or energy to get through my daily work and tasks.\n\nDoctor: Have you been experiencing feelings of worthlessness, or excessive or inappropriate guilt, during this past year and a half?"
  }

========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
No, I haven't really experienced feelings of worthlessness or excessive guilt during this time. It is more that I just feel completely empty and exhausted, rather than blaming myself or feeling worthless.

[
  {
    "role": "system",
    "content": "/no_think\nYou are an assistant analyst connecting the [Symptom Profile] with the doctor's question.\nDetermine which part of the profile (chief complaint, associated symptoms, social history, etc.)\nthe doctor's **last question** is related to.\n\n[Symptom Profile]\n▸ depressive_episode\n  - Depressed Mood: A persistently sunken mood or feeling of depression.\n  - Tearfulness: A state of frequent crying or inability to hold back tears.\n  - Sadness: A state where a sad or gloomy mood is maintained continuously.\n  - Subtypes: Animal Type, Natural Environment Type, Blood-Injection-Injury Type, Situational Type, Other Type\n[S063] Immediate_Fear/Anxiety_Response: The phobic object or situation almost always provokes immediate fear or anxiety. Subtypes: Denial of Medical Risk, Disregard of Symptoms, Dismissal of Concerns\n[S056] Excessive_Anxiety_Worry: Excessive anxiety and worry (expectant apprehension) occurring persistently about a number of events or activities.  Subtypes: Chronic Worry, Generalized Worry, Catastrophizing, Intolerance of Uncertainty\n[S057] Uncontrollable_Worry: The subjective experience of finding it difficult to stop or control the worry.  Subtypes: Failure to Control, Intrusive Worry, Chaining of Worry\n[S058] Restlessness_Tension: Restlessness, or feeling keyed up or on edge.  Subtypes: Feeling Keyed Up, Physical Tension, Nervousness\n[S059] Concentration_Difficulty: Difficulty concentrating or mind going blank.  Subtypes: Interference by Worry, Inability to Sustain Attention, Mind Going Blank\n[S060] Irritability: A state of reacting sensitively or sharply to minor stimuli.  Subtypes: Low Frustration Tolerance, Snappiness, Exhausted Patience\n[S061] Muscle_Tension: A state where muscles are continuously tense, stiff, and accompanied by discomfort.  Subtypes: Neck and Shoulder Tension, Jaw Tension, Generalized Muscle Aches, Chronic Stiffness\n[S062] Specific_Phobic_Fear: Marked fear or anxiety about a specific object or situation that is out of proportion to the actual danger.
